# Board & Shareholder Rights — ORCL

## 0. Data Availability Note

Oracle's FY26 10-K incorporates Part III (Items 10–14: directors, executive compensation, security ownership, related-party transactions, and principal accountant fees) **entirely by reference** to the "2026 Proxy Statement," which the company states it will file with the SEC within 120 days of its May 31, 2026 fiscal year end [FY26 10-K, Part III, Items 10–14]. As of this report's date (2026-08-14), that 2026 proxy has not yet been filed, and no DEF 14A / proxy document of any vintage is in `data/ORCL/`. This is a genuine primary-source gap, not a language issue (CLAUDE.md §27) — the document simply does not exist yet for the current fiscal year.

Per the Partial-Data Rule, this agent used the pool's FY26 10-K signature page (which names the sitting board) and labeled, dated **web sources** — principally Oracle's most recently filed proxy (FY2025 DEF 14A, filed 2025-09-26, covering the board elected at the November 18, 2025 annual meeting) sourced via SEC EDGAR, Oracle's own bylaws/governance-guidelines filings, and the FY2025 annual-meeting voting-results 8-K — to fill governance facts not in the pool. These are CLAUDE.md §4 tier-7 web sources (search-engine syntheses of primary SEC filings, not a direct read of the filing text, since direct fetch of sec.gov returned HTTP 403). They are dated, labeled "Web (unverified)" throughout, and confidence is capped at 2–3 accordingly. Where a fact could not be found in any source, it is marked "Insufficient Data." The 2026 proxy, once filed, should be used to confirm or update every web-sourced fact below.

## 1. Board Composition

|---|---|---|

Henley (Executive Vice Chair, CEO/Chairman 1991–2004), Clayton M. Magouyrk (Co-CEO), Michael D. Sicilia (Co-CEO). Independent: Awo Ablo, Jeffrey S. Berg, Michael J. Boskin, Bruce R. Chizen, Rona A. Fairhead, Tomislav Mihaljevic, Charles W. Moorman IV, Stephen H. Rusckowski | [FY26 10-K, signature page]; [Web: Oracle FY2025 DEF 14A (filed 2025-09-26, sec.gov), search synthesis, 2026-08-14 (unverified) — "the Board will be composed of five non-independent directors...and eight independent directors"] |
| Chair / CEO split? | Split in title only. Ellison holds Executive Chair — a combined board-leadership *and* operating role (he is also Chief Technology Officer) — not an independent, non-executive chair. The CEO seat is separately held by co-CEOs Magouyrk and Sicilia (neither holds the Chair title), but Ellison is the company's founder and largest shareholder at ~40% of the vote [`management-governance/04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md`, 40.21%]. No lead independent director was identified in available sources — Insufficient Data on that specific point. | [FY26 10-K, Item 10 executive bios, "Mr. Ellison...has been our Executive Chair of the Board and Chief Technology Officer since September 2014"] |
| Audit / Comp / Nominating committee independence | Finance & Audit (F&A) Committee and Compensation Committee: web synthesis of the FY2025 DEF 14A states all directors who served on these two committees in fiscal 2025 were independent under NYSE and SEC heightened standards (100% independent membership). Nominating/Governance committee's specific membership and independence were not confirmed from any available source — Insufficient Data on that slice. | [Web: Oracle FY2025 DEF 14A (filed 2025-09-26, sec.gov), search synthesis, 2026-08-14 (unverified) — "all directors who served during fiscal 2025 on the Compensation and F&A Committees were independent under the applicable NYSE and SEC heightened independence standards"] |

## 2. Tenure & Refreshment

| Average director tenure (8 independent directors) | Michael J. Boskin: director since 1994 (~32 yrs); Jeffrey S. Berg: since 1997 (~29 yrs); Bruce R. Chizen: since 2008 (~18 yrs); Charles W. Moorman IV: since 2018 (~8 yrs); Rona A. Fairhead: since 2019 (~7 yrs); Awo Ablo: since 2022 (~4 yrs); Stephen H. Rusckowski: since Nov 2025 (<1 yr); Tomislav Mihaljevic: since May 2026 (<1 yr). Simple average ≈ 12.4 years, heavily skewed by two multi-decade tenures. | [Web: search synthesis of Oracle investor-relations press releases and prior DEF 14A filings (2018–2025 vintages, sec.gov / oracle.com), 2026-08-14 (unverified)] |
| Overboarding / staleness signs | Boskin (~32 yrs) and Berg (~29 yrs) have sat on the board for essentially the entire period Ellison has run or chaired Oracle — a tenure length that most proxy-advisor frameworks (ISS typically flags >9 years, many peers use a 12–15-year soft cap) treat as impairing true independence, regardless of formal NYSE independence status. No confirmed data on other-public-company board seats held by the eight independent directors (overboarding count) — Insufficient Data on that specific metric. One non-independent insider, Safra Catz, sits on the board of Paramount Skydance Corporation (and previously The Walt Disney Company) [FY26 10-K, Item 10 bio]. | [FY26 10-K, Item 10 executive/director bios]; [Web sources cited above] |
| Recent refreshment | Two new independent directors added within the last nine months: Stephen H. Rusckowski (elected Nov 18, 2025) and Tomislav Mihaljevic (elected May 6, 2026) — both added during the same period the CEO seat (Sept 2025) and CFO seat (Apr 2026) also turned over [`management-governance/01_management-and-track-record.md`]. This is genuine refreshment, but it sits alongside — not in place of — the two 29–32-year tenures, and alongside two former CEOs (Catz, Henley) being retained on the board as Executive Vice Chairs rather than departing, which further concentrates insider continuity at the top of the board. | [Web: Oracle investor-relations press releases, "Oracle Names Stephen Rusckowski to the Board of Directors" and Mihaljevic election announcement, 2026-08-14 (unverified, dated)]; [FY26 10-K, Item 10] |

## 3. Related-Party Transactions & Group Leakage

| RPT Type | Amount | % of Revenue | % of PAT | % of Net Worth | Arm's Length? | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| Sales to / purchases from related parties | Not disclosed | — | — | — | Not disclosed | [FY26 10-K, Related Party Disclosures note] |
| Loans / advances / guarantees to related parties | Not disclosed | — | — | — | Not disclosed | [FY26 10-K, Related Party Disclosures note] |
| Royalty / brand / management fees to founder/insider group | None disclosed | — | — | — | N/A | [FY26 10-K, Related Party Disclosures note] |
| Rent / other payments to insider-linked entities | None disclosed | — | — | — | N/A | [FY26 10-K, Related Party Disclosures note] |
| Outstanding receivables from related parties | Not disclosed | — | — | — | Not disclosed | [FY26 10-K, Related Party Disclosures note] |

**No material related-party transactions are disclosed for the current period.** The only related-party note in the audited FY26 10-K identifies equity-method investees (e.g., Ampere Computing, sold to SoftBank Nov-25-2025) as related parties under ASC 850, with no dollar transaction amounts attached — consistent with `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`'s finding that RPTs do not trigger the >25%-of-revenue/expense hard disqualifier. Item 13 ("Certain Relationships and Related Transactions, and Director Independence") — the section that would carry any current-period insider-linked commercial dealings — is incorporated by reference to the unfiled 2026 proxy and cannot be checked directly (see Section 0).

**Historical precedent — the channel is not hypothetical.** Oracle's $9.3bn acquisition of NetSuite Inc. (closed FY17) was litigated for eight years as a related-party self-dealing claim: two derivative suits (filed May and July 2017, consolidated) alleged Ellison and Catz caused Oracle to overpay for NetSuite — a company in which Ellison personally held a large stake — effectively bailing out Ellison's own investment at Oracle shareholders' expense. The Delaware Court of Chancery's post-trial opinion (May 12, 2023) found the evidence fell "well short of proving" Ellison was a conflicted controller in the deal, and the Delaware Supreme Court affirmed that ruling on January 21, 2025 [Web: Dechert LLP case summary and Sidley "Enhanced Scrutiny" case note, both citing the Delaware Court of Chancery post-trial opinion and Delaware Supreme Court affirmance, 2026-08-14 (unverified, but citing primary court opinions)]. This is not a currently open red flag — it was fully litigated and resolved in the company's favor — but it is direct evidence that the founder/board self-dealing channel this section tests for is a real, previously-tested risk at Oracle, not a theoretical one, and that Ellison's ~40% stake plus board presence creates recurring exposure to this class of claim in future large acquisitions.

**Follow-up:** Once the 2026 proxy is filed, check Item 13 for any current insider-linked commercial contracts, family-member employment disclosures, or transactions with entities in which Ellison, Catz, or Henley hold an interest.

## 4. Takeover Defenses & Voting Rights

| Feature | Present? | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Poison pill | Not confirmed present | No "rights plan" or "poison pill" language found anywhere in the FY26 10-K; no evidence found of an active shareholder rights plan in web sources. Absence of evidence is not proof of absence — flagged as Insufficient Data on current status rather than a confirmed "No." | [FY26 10-K, full-text search: no hits]; [Web search, 2026-08-14 (unverified) — no active plan found] |
| Classified / staggered board | No | All 13 directors stand for election annually (single class); Oracle's Amended and Restated Bylaws provide for plurality voting "at all meetings of stockholders for the election of directors," implying annual, non-staggered elections of the full slate. | [Web: Oracle Amended and Restated Bylaws, sec.gov filing, search synthesis, 2026-08-14 (unverified)] |
| Dual-class / unequal voting | No | Oracle has a single class of common stock, one share/one vote; confirmed both by the FY26 10-K cover page (single class of common stock registered) and `management-governance/04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md` | [FY26 10-K, cover page]; [`management-governance/04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md`, finding 04-006] |
| Majority vs plurality director voting | Plurality (weaker standard), overlaid with a resignation policy | Bylaws set a plurality-of-votes-cast standard for director elections. Oracle's Corporate Governance Guidelines add a majority-vote-and-resignation policy: in an uncontested election, a nominee who receives more "withheld" votes than "for" votes must tender a resignation, which the Board then has discretion to accept or reject. This is a softer standard than a true majority-vote requirement (where a nominee failing to win a majority is automatically not elected). | [Web: Oracle Amended and Restated Bylaws and Corporate Governance Guidelines, search synthesis, 2026-08-14 (unverified)] |
| Shareholder ability to call meetings / act by written consent | Call special meeting: yes, at a 20% ownership threshold. Written consent: not confirmed. | Shareholders holding ≥20% of outstanding common stock may call a special meeting. A 2015-era shareholder proposal sought to lower this threshold to 10%; available sources do not show it succeeded, so the 20% threshold appears to remain current — this should be re-verified against the 2026 proxy. No confirmed right to act by written consent in lieu of a meeting was found in any source (Insufficient Data — typical for Delaware companies with a special-meeting right to separately restrict written consent, but this is not confirmed and should not be assumed). | [Web: search synthesis of Oracle bylaws and a historical shareholder proposal, 2026-08-14 (unverified)] |

## 4A. Shareholder Voting & Dilution

| Signal | Detail | Source |

| AGM votes against (say-on-pay) | FY2025 Annual Meeting (Nov 18, 2025): ~1,922,942,188 shares for, ~423,773,929 against, ~3,583,449 abstain (excl. 265,704,606 broker non-votes) → against ≈ 18.0% of votes cast on the say-on-pay resolution. FY2024 Annual Meeting: ~78% for / ~22% against (i.e., worse than FY2025). Both years sit well below the >90% support typical of large-cap say-on-pay votes. | [Web: Oracle 8-K, FY2025 annual-meeting voting results, sec.gov, filed on/around 2025-11-21, search synthesis, 2026-08-14 (unverified)] |
| Proxy-advisor recommendations (ISS / Glass Lewis) | Not directly confirmed from available sources — no specific ISS or Glass Lewis "vote against" or "vote for" recommendation text for the FY2025 or prior Oracle say-on-pay proposals was retrieved. The multi-year pattern of 18–22% opposition is consistent with, but does not prove, historical proxy-advisor concern about Oracle's pay design; treat this link as **Inference, not from filings**. | Insufficient Data (direct recommendation text not retrieved) |
| Institutional opposition to any resolution | Implied only by the 18–22% say-on-pay opposition figures above; no other resolution's vote tally (director elections, auditor ratification) was retrieved. | [Web sources above] |
| Dilution history (preferential allotment, warrants, QIP, ESOP pool) | FY26: $5.0bn Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock (6.50% Series D, converts to common by Jan-15-2029) issued; stock-based compensation of $4,811mn in FY26; diluted share count rose from a 2,766mn low (FY23) to 2,914mn (FY26, +5.3%) as buybacks were cut to near zero — a reversal of the multi-year buyback-driven share-count reduction. | [`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`, "Net share count trajectory" row]; [FY26 10-K, Note 10 (Stockholders' Equity)] |

The 18–22% opposition on say-on-pay over two consecutive annual meetings, while below the module's illustrative >20% single-year trigger in one of the two years and above it in the other, is a persistent, multi-year signal, not a one-off — flagged per RF-SHR-001.

## 5. Minority-Shareholder Protection Read

Board mechanics look reasonable on paper — single class of stock, annual (not staggered) director elections, a nominal 61.5% independent majority, and fully independent Audit and Compensation Committees — but the substance underneath is weaker: the board's own leadership seats concentrate insiders (Ellison as Executive Chair *and* CTO with ~40% of the vote, plus two former CEOs — Catz and Henley — retained as Executive Vice Chairs, plus both sitting co-CEOs), the director-election standard is plurality-with-discretionary-resignation rather than true majority voting, and two of the eight "independent" directors have sat on the board for 29–32 years alongside the founder they are meant to oversee. Say-on-pay opposition of 18–22% in each of the last two annual meetings is a real, recurring minority-shareholder signal that a meaningful bloc of outside investors is not satisfied with pay governance, even though it has never crossed the threshold needed to force a binding change. The single biggest self-dealing risk to minority holders is the founder-insider concentration on the board itself (Ellison/Catz/Henley/Magouyrk/Sicilia hold 5 of 13 seats, and Ellison alone controls ~40% of the vote) combined with a director-election standard that gives a dissatisfied minority no reliable mechanism to remove an entrenched nominee — a structure already tested once, in the NetSuite litigation, and resolved in the company's favor only after eight years of court proceedings. Governance and ownership disclosure for the *current* period is also incomplete because the 2026 proxy (which would carry Item 13's related-party detail and updated committee/voting facts) had not yet been filed as of this report's date. Verdict: **weak / entrenched**, primarily on structure (insider board seats + weak voting standard + long-tenured independents around the founder) rather than on any proven current-period self-dealing.

## Board & Shareholder Rights Score

| Component | Score | Max Score | Evidence |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Board independence | 11 | 20 | 61.5% independent nominally (8/13), but Chair is an executive founder with ~40% of the vote and 5 of 13 seats are current/former executives — see 05-001, 05-002 |
| True independence / tenure / overboarding | 9 | 20 | Two independent directors at 29–32 years tenure alongside the founder; genuine but partial refreshment (2 new directors in 9 months); overboarding count not confirmable — see 05-004, 05-005 |
| Committee quality | 10 | 15 | Audit and Compensation Committees fully independent (positive); Nominating/Governance Committee composition unconfirmed (gap) — see 05-003 |
| RPT and group-leakage protection | 13 | 20 | No current-period material RPT disclosed, but a historical, litigated Ellison-linked self-dealing claim (NetSuite, resolved in the company's favor after 8 years) shows the channel is real; current-period Item 13 detail unavailable pending the 2026 proxy — see 05-006, 05-007, 05-015 |
| Shareholder voting rights | 9 | 15 | Single class / one-share-one-vote and annual (non-staggered) elections are genuine positives; plurality (not majority) director-election standard, a 20% special-meeting threshold, and no confirmed written-consent right or poison-pill status are weaker features — see 05-008 through 05-012 |
| AGM opposition / minority protection | 5 | 10 | 18–22% say-on-pay opposition across the last two annual meetings is a persistent, above-typical minority-shareholder dissent signal — see 05-013 |
| **Total** | **57** | **100** | Mixed/average band (41–60): nominal structural protections exist but are materially weakened by founder/insider board concentration, a weak voting standard, and recurring pay-related minority opposition |

Data-completeness caveat: several inputs above rely on web-sourced syntheses of SEC filings rather than a direct pool read of the (unfiled) 2026 proxy — this caps overall confidence in this score at roughly 3/5 until the 2026 DEF 14A is filed and this agent's findings can be reconciled against it directly.

## management-governance / 06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md

# Candor & Disclosure Quality — ORCL

All figures in USD unless stated otherwise. Fiscal year ends May 31 ("FY2026" = year ended May-31-2026). Reporting standard: US GAAP. This report draws on `01_management-and-track-record.md`, `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md`, and `earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md`, plus its own read of the FY2026 10-K and two verbatim earnings-call transcripts in the data pool.

**Transcript coverage caveat (governs Sections 1 and 4).** The data pool holds two verbatim earnings-call transcripts: FQ3 FY2026 (Mar 10, 2026) and FQ4 FY2026 (Jun 10, 2026) [Oracle Corporation, Q3 2026 Earnings Call, Mar 10, 2026.rtf; Oracle Corporation, Q4 2026 Earnings Call, Jun 10, 2026.rtf]. Both are quarters Oracle beat on revenue and EPS. The pool does **not** contain the FQ2 FY2026 (Dec 10, 2025) call transcript — the single worst quarter in the review period, where Oracle missed revenue, disclosed a $15 billion capex-guidance overshoot, and the stock fell 10.8% in a day [`01_management-and-track-record.md`, Section 4; Oracle Corporation NYSE ORCL Key Developments.xls, 2026-02-05 entry]. A law-firm press release quoting the Dec 10, 2025 earnings release ("now expect[ed] fiscal 2026 CapEx will be about $15 billion higher than [it] forecasted after Q1") is in the pool and is used below strictly for **what was disclosed** (the quantified fact), never for **how it was said** — no paraphrase or press release substitutes for a verbatim transcript on tone or Q&A-evasiveness (per this module's partial-data rule). Tone and Q&A directness in the actual crisis quarter are therefore marked **Insufficient Data** below, and the two available transcripts are used only to test general Q&A style in favorable quarters — a lower and different bar than "candor in bad times," and the two are not conflated.

## 1. Owning the Misses

| Miss (from 01) | How It Was Communicated | Owned or Obscured? | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| FY2026 capex guidance overshoot — management under-forecast capex by ~$15bn within one quarter | Company itself disclosed a specific, dated dollar figure in the Dec 10, 2025 earnings release: it "now expect[ed] fiscal 2026 CapEx will be about $15 billion higher than [it] forecasted after Q1." No vague "headwinds" language was used for this disclosure — this search found zero uses of the word "headwind" anywhere in the two available transcripts or the FY2026 10-K. The number was quantified and released in the same cycle the overshoot became known, not delayed to a later filing | **Owned, on the numbers** — specific, dated, quantified. Tone during the actual Dec 10, 2025 Q&A (defensive vs. plain) is **Insufficient Data** — that transcript is not in the pool | [Oracle Corporation NYSE ORCL Key Developments.xls, 2026-02-05 entry, quoting the Dec 10, 2025 earnings release]; [`01_management-and-track-record.md`, Section 4] |
| FQ2 FY2026 EPS "beat" driven by one-time Ampere/Bloom Energy gains, not operating performance | Management's own FY2026 press release footnote states plainly: "Q4 and FY 2026 results include one-time net investment gains from certain transactions." On the Q4 FY26 call itself, CFO Maxson volunteered the ex-gains number without being asked: "Our non-GAAP EPS was up 27% in U.S. dollars to $7.63, including onetime gains on investment. Excluding these gains, our non-GAAP EPS was $6.83" — a 10.5-point gap she stated in her prepared remarks, not under analyst pressure | **Owned, proactively** — this is the strongest positive candor signal in the record: management named its own inflated number and gave the clean number unprompted | [Q4 FY26 transcript, CFO Maxson prepared remarks]; [`earnings/06_earnings-quality.md`, Section 4] |
| Gross margin compression (-469bps FY2026, guided to step down further in FY2027) | CFO Maxson stated directly on the Q4 call: "gross margin declining driven by impacts from ramping up our data centers and the acceleration in our infrastructure revenue," and for FY2027: it "will step down due to timing for the ramp-up of our data center projects into their full revenue contribution plus impacts from mix." The 10-K MD&A gives the same explanation in writing | **Owned, specific mechanism named** — not attributed to vague "market conditions" | [Q4 FY26 transcript, CFO Maxson]; [`earnings/03_margin-drivers.md`, Section 3] |
| Unresolved securities class action alleging Oracle overstated its AI-deal growth outlook (filed Feb 3, 2026; RF-MGT-005 in `01`) | Disclosed only as required, standard legal-proceedings boilerplate in the 10-K (Note 15): "We believe that we have meritorious defenses against this action, and we will continue to vigorously defend it... we do not believe that it will have a material impact." Neither of the two available earnings-call transcripts (Mar 10 or Jun 10, 2026) contains any analyst question or management comment addressing the substance of the allegation — that the AI-deal growth outlook itself was overstated | **Neither owned nor obscured in an active sense — simply not engaged.** Standard legal boilerplate satisfies disclosure requirements but does not amount to management addressing the substantive question live, which a genuinely candid team facing an outlook-integrity allegation might choose to do | [FY26 10-K, Note 15, "Legal Proceedings — Securities Class Action Regarding Oracle Cloud Infrastructure"]; [Q3 FY26 transcript]; [Q4 FY26 transcript] |
| Worst-quarter (FQ2 FY26) Q&A tone and directness | Not assessable — the Dec 10, 2025 call transcript is absent from the data pool | **Insufficient Data** — do not infer from the two adjacent good-quarter transcripts, which test a different (favorable-results) condition | — |

## 2. Non-GAAP / Adjustment Aggressiveness

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Recurring "one-off" addbacks | Restructuring & other charges excluded from non-GAAP results recurred in consecutive fiscal years under successive named plans: 2024 Restructuring Plan ($374M, FY2025) and a newly initiated 2026 Restructuring Plan ($1,838M, FY2026). A charge labelled "restructuring" that recurs every year under a new plan name is the textbook recurring "one-off" — RF-DISC-002 triggered | earnings/06, Sections 4, 5, 8 |
| GAAP-to-adjusted gap (magnitude / trend) | Operating income gap: 40.4% of reported (FY2026) vs 41.6% (FY2025) — consistently large, not a one-year anomaly. Net income gap: 30.7% (FY2026) vs 38.9% (FY2025). Stock-based compensation alone ($4,811M, 28.2% of GAAP net income) drives over half of the total addback in both years, and is excluded from non-GAAP results in full every year | earnings/06, Section 7 |
| Does adjusted framing flatter reality? | Yes, on two specific dimensions the module's own scan already caught: (1) the GAAP effective tax rate (12.6%) sits well below the company's own non-GAAP rate (19.9%), a gap driven mainly by stock-based-compensation-related tax benefits that scale with the share price, not operating performance; (2) a $2,493M pre-tax Ampere Computing sale gain plus Bloom Energy warrant gains drove roughly a third of FY2026's reported 36% net-income-available-to-common growth. Management itself backs the investment gains out of its own forward growth math (18% vs. 34% headline non-GAAP EPS growth) — a genuine mitigating factor, since the flattering treatment is disclosed and self-corrected, not hidden | earnings/06, Sections 8, 10 |

**New metric introduced the same year capex ballooned.** Oracle introduced a company-defined "net cash outlay for capital expenditures" metric on the Q4 FY2026 call — $70bn guided for FY2027, versus a gross capex figure of $90–95bn once ~$20–25bn of customer prepayments and timing impacts are added back [Q4 FY26 transcript, CFO Maxson prepared remarks]. The netting is disclosed with a reconciliation (not hidden), and it tracks a real cash mechanic (large customer prepayments, which `earnings/06` independently verified are cash-backed, not fabricated). But introducing a new, smaller-looking headline capex number in the exact year gross capex nearly tripled ($21.2bn → $55.7bn) is a framing choice worth flagging, not a neutral change in convention.

## 3. Disclosure Transparency

Segment and operating disclosure is genuinely specific, not boilerplate: management discloses global GPU utilization (97.5%), a stated RPO-conversion schedule (12% recognized in the next 12 months, 34% in months 13–36), and site-by-site capacity-delivery percentages for its five largest data centers [Q4 FY26 transcript, CEO Magouyrk]. Results are filed 10 days after quarter-end — management calls this out itself as the fastest in the S&P 500 — and no delayed-filing or delayed-disclosure pattern was found in the pool (RF-REG-002 not triggered). Disclosure got LESS transparent in one specific respect worth watching: capex maintenance-vs-growth spend is not broken out (unchanged, a standing gap, not new), and the new "net cash outlay for capex" metric (Section 2) replaces gross capex as the headline figure precisely when gross capex became the least flattering number to lead with.

## 4. Tone in Bad Times

**Not directly assessable for the actual worst quarter** — the Dec 10, 2025 (FQ2 FY26) transcript, the quarter that missed revenue, disclosed the $15bn capex overshoot, and cost the stock 10.8%, is absent from the pool. What is assessable: the two available transcripts (both strong-beat quarters) show management answering hard, specific questions directly rather than deflecting — e.g., on the Q4 FY26 call, when analyst John DiFucci noted Oracle "spent a little bit more this quarter on CapEx than we expected" and asked about rising component costs, CEO Magouyrk gave a specific, technical answer (fixed-price vs. floating-price contract mechanics tied to cost certainty) rather than a generic reassurance. That is evidence of a generally non-evasive Q&A style, but it is evidence from favorable quarters and cannot stand in for how the team handled the actual bad print — "anyone is candid when results are good" is exactly the gap this report cannot close from the available pool.

## 5. Candor Read

Oracle's disclosure is specific and numbers-heavy where it counts — the $15bn capex miss, the ex-one-off EPS figure, and the gross-margin mechanism were each given a dated, quantified explanation rather than vague language, and the CFO volunteered the least flattering number ($6.83 ex-gains vs. $7.63 headline) unprompted on the Q4 call. Set against that: restructuring charges have recurred as "one-offs" under successive named plans for two straight years, the non-GAAP-to-GAAP gap has stayed above 40% of operating income for two years running, a new company-defined capex metric appeared the same quarter gross capex nearly tripled, and prepared-remarks language ("tremendous quarter," "overdelivering... across the board") describes a beat streak that `earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md` shows is closer to a coin flip (2 of 4 quarters clean on both revenue and EPS once one-off gains are stripped) — a real gap between the verbal framing and the underlying numbers. The single biggest candor signal is a genuine gap in the evidence, not a finding: the pool has no verbatim record of how management spoke in the one quarter that actually went badly, so this report cannot confirm candor was maintained under real pressure — only that the numbers, when disclosed, were disclosed specifically. Combined with the unresolved securities-fraud allegation (RF-MGT-005, routed from `01`) that management has not substantively addressed beyond legal boilerplate, this compounds rather than nets out against the positive specificity evidence. Verdict: **mixed**.

RF-DISC-002 (recurring "one-off" / aggressive non-GAAP add-backs)

RF-DISC-001 (commentary contradicting the numbers)

## Disclosure Candor Score

| Component | Score | Max Score | Evidence |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Owning misses | 16 | 25 | Capex miss and ex-gains EPS both disclosed with specific, dated dollar figures and no euphemistic language (Section 1); capped because the worst-quarter (FQ2 FY26) verbatim Q&A tone is not assessable from the pool, and the unresolved integrity allegation is not substantively engaged |
| KPI consistency | 14 | 20 | RPO, cloud-growth, and utilization metrics reported consistently quarter to quarter; docked for the new "net cash outlay for capex" metric introduced the same quarter gross capex nearly tripled |
| Non-GAAP / adjustment discipline | 9 | 20 | Recurring restructuring "one-offs" under successive plans (RF-DISC-002), SBC excluded in full every year (28.2% of GAAP net income), 7.3pt GAAP/non-GAAP tax-rate gap; partial credit because the largest single flattering item (Ampere/Bloom gains) is voluntarily backed out of management's own forward guidance |
| Q&A transparency | 10 | 15 | Direct, technical answers observed in the two available (favorable-quarter) transcripts; capped hard because Q&A in the actual worst quarter is Insufficient Data |
| Disclosure timeliness | 9 | 10 | Results filed 10 days post quarter-end, fastest in the S&P 500 per company's own (verifiable) claim; no delayed-disclosure pattern found |
| Specificity vs boilerplate | 7 | 10 | Genuinely specific operational KPIs (97.5% utilization, site-by-site delivery %, RPO conversion schedule); docked for sales-pitch-heavy prepared remarks whose framing outruns the clean beat-rate numbers (RF-DISC-001) |
| **Total** | **65** | **100** | Mixed: strong specificity and proactive ex-gains disclosure, offset by two years of recurring "one-off" adjustments, a promotional-vs-numbers gap, and a genuine data gap on the one quarter that would test candor under real pressure |
